COMPLAINT AND JURY DEMAND 


COME NOW, Plaintiffs Mary Forsyth, Robert and 
Marrietta Cade, Willie Andrews, Mary Lou Buehler, Helen 
Staves, Randolph Bratton, Searle Auto Glass, Inc., d/b/ 
Best Glass Co., and W. E. Krummel, Sr., by and through 
their counsel of record, Jones, Jones, Close & Brown, 
Chartered, and Waite, Schneider, Bayless & Chesley Co. 
L.P.A., and for their causes of action against Defendants 
complain, aver and allege as follows: 


JURISDICTION 


1. This action arises under the laws of the United States 
and includes claims under the Employee Retirement Income 
Security Act (“‘ERISA’’), 29 U.S.C. § 1001, ef seg., the 
antitrust laws of the United States, 15 U.S.C. § 1 ef seq., 
and the law of the State of Nevada. Subject matter jurisdic- 
tion in conferred upon this Court by 29 U.S.C. § 1132, 15 
U.S.C. § 15, 28 U.S.C. §1237, and the doctrine of ancillary 


and pendent jurisdiction. 


2. Venue of this action is proper in this Court pursuant to 
15 U.S.C. § 15, 28 U.S.C. § 1391 and 29 U.S.C. § 1132. 


BACKGROUND 

3. Plaintiffs are individuals and entities utilizing health 
care services and/or the health insurance plan of Defendant, 
Humana, Inc. (‘‘Humana’’), and its wholly owned 
subsidiary Defendant, Humana Health Insurance of Nevada, 
Inc. (‘‘Humana-Insurance’’). Humana owns and operates 
Humana Hospital-Sunrise, an acute care facility located in 
Clark County, Nevada. Group Health Insurance, Inc. 
(‘‘Group-Insurance’’) is a wholly owned subsidiary of 
Humana and it retains beneficial interest in 100% of the 
stock of Humana-Insurance. Plaintiffs and the class are suing 
the Defendants for the damages they sustained as a result of

provisions, Ms. Forsyth paid $2,300.00 out of her own 
pocket for that hospitalization. 

5. Plaintiffs Robert and Marrietta Cade, 4433 West Lake 
Mead Boulevard, Las Vegas, Nevada have been insured with 
Humana-Insurance since 1985. Both Robert and Marrietta 
Cade worked for many years at the Las Vegas Sun. Mr. 


December 1988. Mr. Cade was hospitalized at Humana 
Hospital-Sunrise between October 7, 1987 and October 17, 
1987. Mr. Cade incurred a hospital bill of $16,437.66. The 
Cades paid all appropriate co-payments for his hospitaliza- 
tion. The Cades were at all times relevant Humana-Insur- 
Humana Care Plus paying a monthly premium of $355.00 
month. 


E 


JA-4 


6. Plaintiff Willie Andrews, 804 McWilliams Street, Las 
Vegas, Nevada 89106, has been insured with Humana Care 
Plus since May 1985. Mr. Andrews was hospitalized from 
November 4, 1986 through November 9, 1986. At all times 
relevant Mr. Andrews was employed with the Clark County 
School District. Mr. Andrews, under his co-pay provisions, 
was obligated to pay a percentage of all of his hospitalization 
costs as well as the premium payments paid by him through 
his employer. 


7. Plaintiff Mary Lou Buehler, 210 Meyers Avenue, 
Henderson, Nevada 89015, has been insured with Humana- 
Insurance since 1984. She is insured wey her husband’s 
employer, Cashman Cadillac. Through her husband an 
optional premium is paid of approximately $140.00 per 


month for her coverage as a dependent. In September 1987 
and again in October of 1988 Mrs. Buehler was hospitalized 


for a total of approximately fourteen days. Her billings for 
those hospitalizations were $23,293.94 and $2,736.85, 
respectively. Under the co-payment provision of her policy, 
she was obligated to pay 20% of the costs of her hospitaliza- 
tion, over and above the premium payments. 


8. Plaintiff Helen Staves, 37 East Stewart, Unit 50, Las 
Vegas, Nevada 89110, has been insured with Humana- 
Insurance since July 1988. In December 1988 Ms. Staves 
was hospitalized at Humana Hospital-Sunrise. Pursuant to 
the co-payment provisions under her policy, she is required 
to pay 20% of the costs of that hospitalization. Her expenses 
incurred for that hospitalization were $14,764.25. At all 
times relevant, Mrs. Staves was employed at the Dunes 
Hotel and made separate monthly premium payments of 
$40.00 per pay period for her policy of health insurance 
with Humana-Insurance. 


9. Plaintiff Randolph Bratton, P.O. Box 175, State Route 
38, Las Vegas, Nevada 89124 has been insured with 


JA-5 


Humana-Insurance since 1985. Between 1985 and August of 
1987, Mr. Bratton was employed by the Las Vegas Sun. 
Since August 1987, Mr. Bratton has been employed by the 
Clark County School District. Mr. Bratton remains in the 
employ of Clark County School District to this date. At all 
times relevant, Mr. Bratton was an insured of Humana- 
Insurance. On February 12, 1988, Mr. Bratton was admitted 
to Humana Hospital-Sunrise. At that time, day surgery was 
performed and he incurred a hospital bill of approximately 
$1,400.00. Mr. Bratton remains obligated on that amount as 
Humana-Insurance has denied the claim of payment as a pre- 
existing condition. 

10. Plaintiff Searle Auto Glass, Inc., doing business as 
Best Glass Co. (‘‘Best Glass’’), 1225 S. Commerce Street, 
Las Vegas, Nevada 89102, is a Nevada corporation in good 
standing. Best Glass has utilized Humana-Insurance to 
provide health insurance to its employees between the years 
1985 and 1988, inclusive. Best Glass group number with 
Humana-Insurance is 82801-18351-001. When Best Glass 
first became an insured with Humana-Insurance, the rates 
were very reasonable, $64.23 per employee per billing 
period. In 1986 this rate increased by 3% and in 1987 the 
rate jumped 13%. Finally, when Best Glass decided not to 
renew with Humana-Insurance, it sought a premium increase 
of 41% to continue coverage of employees of Best Glass. At 
no time did Humana-Insurance ever inform Best Glass that it 
was able to negotiate favorable hospitalization rates for its 
employees using Humana Hospital-Sunrise for hospitaliza- 
tions. 

11. Plaintiff W. E. Krummel, Sr., 1225 S. Commerce 
Street, Las Vegas, Nevada 89102, was an insured with 
Humana-Insurance and an employee of Best Glass between 
1985 and 1988, inclusive. 


JA-6 


12. The named Plaintiffs are: employers who have 
provided insurance to their employees through Humana- 
Insurance, insureds of Humana-Insurance, claimants under 
the health insurance policies issued by Humana-Insurance 
(‘‘Humana Care Plus’’), and individuals who have utilized 
the health care services of Humana. 


13. Defendant Humana-Insurance is a Nevada corporation 
in good standing conducting business in the County of 
Clark, State of Nevada and is a wholly owned subsidiary of 
Humana (through Group-Health). 


14. ‘‘Group-Insurance’’ is a Delaware corporation holding 
beneficial interest in all of the stock of Humana-Insurance 
and is itself a wholly owned subsidiary of Humana. 


15. Defendant Humana is a Kentucky corporation licensed 
to do business in the State of Nevada. 


16. The true names and capacities, whether individual, 
corporate, associate, or otherwise, of Defendants herein 
designated as DOE CORPORATIONS I through V, 
inclusive, are unknown to Plaintiffs at this time, who there- 
fore sue said Defendants by such fictitious names. Plaintiffs 
are informed and believe and thereon allege that each of said 
Defendants is responsible in some manner for the events and 
happenings and proximately caused the injuries and damages 
herein alleged. Plaintiffs will seek leave to amend this 
Complaint to allege their true names and capacities when 
and as ascertained. 


CLASS ACTION ALLEGATIONS 
17. This section is brought by Plaintiffs as a class action 
on their own behalf and on behalf of the following classes 
and all others similarly situated, under the provisions of 
Rule 23(a) and Rule 23(b)(1)(B), (2) and (3) of the Federal 
Rules of Civil Procedure, for damages, equitable relief and 
declaratory relief, including costs and attorneys’ fees. 


JA-7 


(a) A class consisting of employers and individuals 
contracting with Humana-Insurance and paying 
premiums on behalf of themselves and/or their 
employees; and 

(b) A class consisting of individuals who were hospital- 
ized at Humana Hospital-Sunrise and who were 
insured with Humana-Insurance. 


18. The classes are represented by Plaintiffs in this action 
of which Plaintiffs are themselves members, and consist of 
all those insured by Humana-Insurance and/or or who 
received inpatient hospitalization at Humana Hospital- 
Sunrise and/or who made premium payments to Humana- 
Insurance at all times relevant hereto. 


19. The members of the class of Plaintiffs are so numer- 
ous that joinder is impractical. The exact number of the 
members of the class is unknown to Plaintiffs, but may be 
determined from records maintained by Defendants Humana 
and Humana-Insurance. It is estimated that there may be tens 
of thousands of plaintiffs. In many instances the members of 
the class are unaware that claims exist or are about to be 
brought, or are unaware of the facts giving rise to such 
claims, or having know!edyze of such claims, or having 
knowledge of such claims, had, in many instances, sustained 
damages in such an amount that when taken individually are 
too small to justify the enormous expense and effort required 
to sue separately. Nevertheless, the damages and class 
members as a group are substantial and significant to justify 
the taking of legal action. 

20. There are common questions of law and fact in the 
action that relate to and affect the rights of each member of 
the class and the relief sought in common to the entire class. 
Each Plaintiff asserts that Humana and Humana-Insurance in 
1985, 1986, 1987 and 1988 operated a scheme to unlawfully 


JA-8 


and/or inequitably profit from hospitalizations at Humana 
Hospital-Sunrise. 

21. This action is properly maintained as a class action 
inasmuch *s the questions of law and fact common to all 
members of the class predominate over any questions 
affecting only individual members, and the class action is 
superior to other available methods for the fair and efficient 
adjudication of this controversy. Among the questions of law 
or fact common to the classes are the following: 


(a) Whether Defendants breached their contractual 
obligations to Plaintiffs to provide hospital services 
at a reasonable price? 


(b) Whether Humana-Insurance breached its obligation 
to its Plaintiff insureds to pass along reduced costs 
in the form of reduced premiums? 


(c) Whether Defendants participated in a scheme to 
unlawfully and/or inequitably funnel monies from 
Humana to Humana-Insurance in 1987 and 1988 in 
violation of state law and in breach of their obliga- 
tions to plaintiffs? 


(d) Whether Defendants unlawfully and/or inequitably 
failed to reduce co-payments and deductible pay- 
ments of plaintiffs for hospitalizations at Humana- 
Sunrise? 

(e) Whether Defendants by their conduct violated 
federal and state antitrust laws? 


(f) Whether Defendants have been unjustly enriched by 
the scheme described herein? 

(g) Whether Plaintiffs and the class have been damaged 
and the extent of those damages? 


22. The claims of Plaintiffs, who are represented as of the 
class herein, are typical of the claims of the class, in that the 


JA-9 


claims of all members of the class, including Plaintiffs, 
depend upon a showing of the acts and omissions of 
Defendants giving rise to the right of Plaintiffs to the relief 
sought herein. 

23. The named Plaintiffs are the representative parties for 
the class, and are able to, and will, fairly and adequately 
protect the interest of the class. The attorneys for Plaintiffs 
are experienced and knowledgeable in the field and have 
successfully represented claimants in other litigation. 

24. This action is properly maintained as a class action 
under Fed. R. Civ. P. 23(b)(1)(B) because prosecution of 
separate actions by individual members of the class would 
create a risk of adjudications with respect to individual 
members of the class that would, as a practical matter, 
dispose of the interest of the other members not parties to 
the adjudications, or which substantially impair or impede 
their ability to protect their interests. 

25. This action is properly maintained as a class action 
under FRCP 23(b)(2) inasmuch as the Defendants, all of 
whom oppose the class, have acted or refused to act, as 
herein more specifically allege, on grounds which are 
applicable to the class and have by reason of such conduct, 
made appropriate final equitable relief and declaratory relief 
with respect to the entire class, as sought in this action. 

FIRST CLAIM FOR RELIEF 
(Breach of ERISA Fiduciary Duties) 


26. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every allega- 


tion contained in paragraphs 1 through 25 of the Complaint 
as though fully set forth at this point. 


27. Many of the members of the class are employers or 


employees obtaining or providing health insurance benefits 
under an employee benefit plan as defined under 29 U.S.C. 
1001, ef seq. 


JA-10 


28. Defendant Humana-Insurance is the insurer of a class 
of Plaintiffs through its insurance plan, Humana Care Plus. 
Under the Humana Care Plus policy, Humana-Insurance has 
fiduciary duties to Plaintiffs who fall under ERISA protec- 
tion. 29 U.S.C. §§ 1104 and 1105. 


29. Under 29 U.S.C. 1001, ef seg., Humana and 
Humana-Insurance, as fiduciaries of Plaintiffs, have a duty 
to provide health insurance at a reasonable cost. 


30. Humana and Humana-Insurance entered into an 
agreement to reduce hospitalization costs for Humana- 
Insurance plaintiffs. 


31. Defendant Humana-Insurance has breached its 
fiduciary duties to all Plaintiffs protected by ERISA in that it 
has failed to pass along the reduced costs it was able to 
negotiate with Humana in the form of reduced premiums and 
co-payments. 

32. Plaintiffs have been damaged as a result of the breach 
of its fiduciary duties by Humana and Humana-Insurance. 


33. It has become necessary for Plaintiffs to retain the law 
firms of Jones, Jones, Close & Brown, Chartered and 
Waite, Schneider, Bayless & Chesley Co., L.P.A. to 
prosecute this action, and Plaintiffs are entitled to recover 
reasonable attorneys’ fees therefor. 29 U.S.C. § 1132(g). 

SECOND CLAIM FOR RELIEF 
(Constructive Trust) 
34. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every allega- 


tion contained in paragraphs 1 through 25 of the Complaint 
as though fully set forth at this point. 


35. The Defendants have retained profits in excess of 
$10,000,000.00 as a result of the scheme described herein. 


JA-11 


36. Such monies were unlawfully obtained in contraven- 
tion of Assembly Bill 289, as passed by the 1987 Nevada 
Legislature and signed into law by the Governor of the State 
of Nevada (‘‘AB 289°’), and further are in violation of the 
Defendants’ duties to the Plaintiffs. 29 U.S.C. 1001, et seq. 


37. It would be wrongful for Humana and Humana- 
Insurance to retain monies, in excess of those they might 
otherwise have been entitled to, when such monies have 
been obtained through the breach of federal and state law 
and Humana-Insurance agreement with Plaintiffs. 


38. A constructive trust should be imposed on the 
Defendants which requires turnover of all monies obtained 
through the scheme described herein. 

39. Plaintiffs should be named the beneficiaries of the 
constructive trust awarded by this Court. 


40. It has become necessary for Plaintiffs to retain the law 
firms of Jones, Jones, Close & Brown, Chartered and 
Waite, Schneider, Bayless & Chesley Co., L.P.A. to 
prosecute this action, and Plaintiffs are entitled to recover 
reasonable attorneys’ fees therefor. 


THIRD CLAIM FOR BELIEF 


(Breach of Contract) 


41. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every allega- 
tion contained in Paragraphs 1 through 25 of the General 
sentinne =. 

42. Defendant Humana-Insurance is the insurer of a class 
of Plaintiffs through its insurance plan, Humana Care Plus. 
Under the Humana Care Plus policy and the laws of the 
State of Nevada, Humana-Insurance is obligated to pass 
along reduced costs to its insureds in the form of reduced 
premiums. 


JA-12 


43. Humana and Humana-Insurance entered into an 
agreement to reduce hospitalization costs for Humana- 
44. Defendant Humana-Insurance has breached its 
obligation to Plaintiffs under the Humana Care Plus policy 
in that it has failed to pass along the reduced costs it was 
able to negotiate with Humana in the form of reduced 
premiums. 


45. Defendant Humana-Insurance has breached its 
agreement with Plaintiffs causing damages in the form of 
hospitalization stays at Humana Hospital-Sunrise. 

46. As a result of defendants breach Plaintiffs have suf- 
fered damages in excess of $10,000,000.00. 


47. It has become necessary for Plaintiffs to retain the law 
firms of Jones, Jones, Close & Brown, Chartered and 
Waite, Schneider, Bayless & Chesley Co., L.P.A. to 
prosecute this action, and Plaintiffs are entitled to recover 
reasonable attorneys’ fees therefor. 


FOURTH CLAIM FOR RELIEF 
(Unfair Claims Practices) 

48. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every 
allegation contained in Paragraphs 1 through 46 of the 
Complaint as though fully set forth at this point. 

49. Humana-Insurance is engaged in the business of 
insurance in the State of Nevada and policies of insurance 
were issued and delivered to Plaintiffs and the members of 


the class were employees or insureds within the State of 
Nevada. 


50. Humana-Insurance committed the following acts in 
violation of the Nevada Unfair Claims Practices Act, 
686A.310 in that it: 


JA-13 


(a) Failed to pass along premium reductions to their in- 
sureds; 
(b) Failed to properly process claims of its insureds; 
(c) Failed to notify insureds that they had overpaid their 
co-payments and deductibles; and 
(d) Failed to make prompt and reasonable settlements of 
all claims presented. 
51. The above acts constitute violations of HRS 686A.310 
and there is a private right of action for all such acts com- 
mitted in 1987 and 1988. 


52. Plaintiffs and the members of the class have suffered 
damages in an amount in excess of $10,000,000.00. 


53. It has been necessary for Plaintiffs to retain the law 
firms of Jones, Jones, Close & Brown, Chartered and 
Waite, Schneider, Bayless & Chesley Co., L.P.A. to 
prosecute this action, and Plaintiffs are entitled to recover 
reasonable attorneys’ fees therefor. 

FIFTH CLAIM FOR RELIEF 


(Breach of the Implied Covenant of Good Faith 
and Fair Dealings) 

54. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every 
allegation contained in Paragraphs 1 through 52 of the 
Complaint as though fully set forth at this point. 

55. Implied in the Humana-Insurance health insurance 
policies with Plaintiffs is the covenant of good faith and fair 

56. Humana-Insurance has breached the covenant of good 
faith and fair dealings in that it has failed to properly handle 
the claims of Plaintiffs and notify Plaintiffs of reduced 

jums and cost savings that were agreed to by its parent 
corporation, Humana. 


JA-14 


57. Humana-Insurance’s conduct as described herein is 
willful, wanton, malicious and/or is in conscious reckless 
disregard for the rights or Plaintiffs, its insureds. 


58. As the proximate result of the conduct of Humana- 
Insurance, Plaintiffs have been damaged generally and 
specially in an amount in excess of $10,000,000.00 and are 
entitled to punitive damages. 

59. It has been necessary for Plaintiffs to retain the law 
firms of Jones, Jones, Close & Brown, Chartered and 
Waite, Schneider, Bayless & Chesley Co., L.P.A. to 
prosecute this action, and Plaintiffs are entitled to recover 
reasonable attorneys’ fees therefor. 


SIXTH CLAIM FOR RELIEF 
(Anti Trust) 


60. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every 
allegation contained in paragraphs 1 through 25 of the 
Complaint as though fully set forth at this point. 

61. Defendants Humana and Humana-Insurance provided 
services within the State of Nevada, County of Clark, to 
Plaintiffs. Defendants are also engaged in the business of 
interstate commerce. Defendants offer their services both to 
in-state and out-of-state patients and/or insureds. 


62. Humana-Insurance, although a wholly owned 
subsidiary of Group-Health, which in turn is wholly owned 
by Humana, was at all times relevant hereto treated as an 
independent and separate entity by Humana, and entered into 
an arm’s length agreement with Humana. Humana and 
Humana-Insurance conspired to operate a scheme which 
includes the following components: 
(a) Initially offering extremely low premium rates for 
the Humana Care Plus policy with the intention of 
penetration was obtained; 


(c) Creating a boycott of all other acute care providers 
(by effectively forcing all Humana-Insurance in- 
sureds to be treated at Humana Hospital-Sunrise) in 
the Southern Nevada area for insureds of Humana- 


constitutes a conspiracy which is intended to and does in fact 
unreasonably restrain trade and commerce in the relevant 
geographic market in violation of 15 U.S.C. §§ 1 and 2 and 
HRS 598A.910, et seq., all to the damage and detriment of 
65. Plaintiffs are entitled to receive costs of suit and 
reasonable attorneys’ fees and Plaintiffs’ damages are to be 
trebled for the conduct described herein. 15 U.S.C. § 15. 


SEVENTH CLAIM FOR RELIEF 
(Unjust Enrichment) 

66. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every allega- 
tion contained in paragraph 1 through 64 of the Complaint 
as though fully set forth at this point. 

67. As a result of the Defendants’ unlawful, and/or ineq- 
uitable scheme heretofore alleged, Plaintiffs conferred a 
monetary benefit upon the Defendants. Defendants appreci- 
ated and realized the monetary benefit and Defendants ac- 
cepted and retained the monetary benefit under circum- 


JA-16 


stances such that it would be inequitable for the Defendants 
to retain the monetary benefit without returning the benefit 
to Plaintiffs. As a result of the scheme, the Defendants were 
unjustly enriched. 

68. As a direct and proximate result of the unjust enrich- 
ment, Plaintiffs have suffered damages in an amount in 
excess of $10,000,000.00. 


69. It has been necessary for Plaintiffs to retain the law 
firms of Jones, Jones, Close & Brown, Chartered and 
Waite, Schneider, Bayless & Chesley Co., L.P.A. to prose- 
cute this action, and Plaintiffs are entitled to recover reason- 
able attorneys’ fees therefor. 


EIGHTH CLAIM FOR RELIEF 
(Declaratory Relief) 
70. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every allega- 


tion contained in paragraphs 1 through 25 of the Complaint 
as though fully set forth at this point. 


71. A declaratory judgment pursuant to Rule 57 of the 
Federal Rules of Civil Procedure and 28 U.S.C. § 2201, as 


well as NRCP 57, therefore, is needed to judicially declare 
the rights of Plaintiffs and all class members under the 
Humana-Insurance policy, to prevent further abuses that 
threaten Plaintiffs and all class members’ ability to obtain 
medical insurance at reasonable premiums and to pay rea- 
sonable co-payments, where applicable. 

WHEREFORE, Plaintiffs pray for judgment against De- 
fendants, jointly and/or severally, where applicable in each 
claim for relief as follows: 


1. An amount in excess of $10,000,000.00 on the First 
Claim for Relief. 


2. A constructive trust to be imposed upon the Defendants 
in the amount of all monies received as a result of the 


JA-17 


scheme, with Plaintiffs to be named as the beneficiaries of 
the trust on the Second Claim for Relief. 


3. An amount in excess of $10,000,000.00 on the Third 
Claim for Relief. 


4. For general, special, compensatory and punitive

[Section 3 3-2] 


ACTIVE SERVICE. An Employee will be considered in 
Active Service with the Employer on a day which is one of 
the Employer’s scheduled work days if he is performing in 
the usual way all of the regular duties of his work for the 
Employer on a full-time basis on that day, either at one of 
the Employer’s places of business or at some location to 
which the Employer’s business requires him to travel. An 
Employee will be deemed in Active Service on a day which 
is not one of the Employer’s scheduled work days only if he 
was in Active Service on the preceding schedule work days. 


COVERED CHARGES OR COVERED EXPENSES. The 
terms mean charges or expenses incurred for medical care to 
the extent they are covered by this policy and for which 
benefits are payable. 

EMPLOYEE. The term Employee means a full-time 
employee of the Employer, but does not include employees 


JA-25 


who are part-time or temporary or who normally work less 
than 20 hours a week for the Employer. 


EMPLOYER. The term Employer means the Policyholder 
and all Affiliated Employers shown in the ‘‘Eligibility for 
Insurance’’ section. 
[Section 4 

(NV-Rev. 8-34) 4-1] 


DEPENDENT. The term Dependent means: 
(1) the lawful spouse of the Employee; and 
(2) any unmarried child who is 

(a) less than 19 years old; 


(b) 19 years but less than 25 years old, enrolled in a 
school as a full-time student and primarily support- 
ed by the Employee; 


(c) 19 or more years old and primarily supported by 
the Employee and incapable of self-sustaining 
employment by reason of mental or physical 
handicap. At the time of claim under the policy for 
a child who is handicapped as defined in this 
paragraph, the policyholder must submit proof to 
the Insurance Company that the child is and has 
continued to be handicapped as defined by this 
paragraph. 

The term child means a child born to the Employee and a 
child legally adopted by the Employee. It also means a step- 
child of the Employee living with the Employee. 

Anyone who is eligible for the insurance as an Employee 
will not be considered as a Dependent. 

No one may be considered as a dependent of more than one 
Employee. 


JA-26 
[Section 4 4-2] 


HOSPITAL CONFINEMENT OF CONFINED IN A 
HOSPITAL. A person will be considered confined in a 
Hospital if he is: 
(1) a registered bed patient in a hospital upon the recom- 
mendation of a Physician; 
(2) an out-patient in a hospital because of surgery; or 
(3) receiving emergency care in a hospital for an Injury on 
his first visit as an out-patient within 72 hours after the 
Injury is received. 
HOSPITAL. The term Hospital means: 
(1) an institution licensed as a hospital, which: 


(a) maintains, on the premises, all facilities necessary 
for medical and surgical treatment; and facilities 
controlled by the hospital; 


(b) provides such treatment on an in-patient basis, for 


compensation, under the supervision of Physicians; 
and 


(c) provides 24-hour service by Registered Graduate 
Nurses; or 


(2) an institution which qualifies as a hospital, a psychiat- 
ric hospital, a tuberculosis hospital, a provider of 
rehabilitative services and a provider of services under 
Medicare, if such institutions is accredited as a hospi- 
tal by the Joint Commission on the Accreditation of 
Hospitals, the American Osteopathic Hospital Associa- 
tion, or the Commission on the Accreditation of 
Rehabilitative Facilities. 


(3) an Ambulatory Surgical Center. 
The term Hospital will not include: 


JA-27 


(a) an institution which is primarily a place for rest, a 
place for the aged, a place for the treatment of 
alcohol or drug abuse, or a nursing home; 


[Section 4 4-3] 
PARTICIPATING HOSPITAL. The term Participating 
~ 


bulatory Surgical Center means a licensed public or private 


BED AND BOARD. The term Bed and Board includes all 


meals and for all general services and activities needed for 


the patient’s diagnosed sickness or injury. It includes: 


JA-28 


(1) any charges, except charges for Bed and Board, made 
by a Hospital on its own behalf for medical services 
and supplies actually used during Hospital Confine- 
ment; 

(2) any charges, by whomever made, for licensed ambu- 
lance service to or from the nearest Hospital where the 
needed medical care and treatment can be provided; 
and 


(3) any charges, by whomever made, for the administra- 
tion of anesthetics during Hospital Confinement. 
The term Necessary Services and Supplies will not include 
any charges for special nursing fees, dental fees or medical 
fees. 


PARTIAL HOSPITALIZATION SERVICES. The term 
Partial Hospitalization Services is defined as those services 
offered by a program accredited by the Joint Commission on 
Accreditation of Hospitals or in compliance with equivalent 
standards. Alcohol rehabilitation programs accredited by the 
Joint Commission on Accreditation of Hospitals or approved 
by the state and licensed drug abuse rehabilitation programs 
shall also be considered Partial Hospitalization Services. 

[Section 4 4-4] 


NURSE. The term Nurse a Registered Graduate 
Nurse, a Licensed Practical Nurse or a Licensed Vocational 
Nurse who has the right to use the abbreviation “‘R.N.’’, 
**L.P.H.”’, or ““L.V.N.”’. 


PERSONAL EXPENSE LIMIT. The personal expense limit 
is the amount of Covered Medical expense that must be paid 
by each person or family in a calendar year. 

PHYSICIAN. The term Physician means a licensed medical 
practitioner who is practicing within the scope of his license 
and who is licensed to prescribe and administer drugs or to 


JA-29 


perform surgery. It will also include any other licensed 
medical practitioner whose services are required to be 
covered by law in the locality where the policy is issued if 
he is: 

(1) operating within the scope of his license; and 


(2) performing a service for which benefits are provided 
under this plan when performed by a Physician. 
PSYCHOLOGIST. The term Psychologist means a person 
who is licensed or certified as a clinical psychologist. Where 
no licensure or certification exists, the term Psychologist 
means a person who is considered qualified as a clinical 
psychologist by a recognized psychological association. It 
whose services are required to be covered by law in the 

locality where the policy is issued if he is: 

(1) operating within the scope of his license; and 

(2) performing a service for which benefits are provided 
under this plan when performed by a Psychologist.

SPECIAL CARE FACILITY. The term Special Care Facili- 

ty means a licensed institution (other than a Hospital, as 

defined) which: 

(1) talizes in physical rehabilitation: 


(2) qualifies as a skilled nursing facility and a provider of 
services under Medicare; 


but only if that institution: (a) maintains on the premises all 
facilities necessary for medical treatment; (b) provides such 


treatment, for compensation, under the supervision of Physi- 
cians; and (c) provides Nurses’ services. 


JA-30 


MEDICAID. The term Medicaid means a state program of 
medical aid for needy persons established under Title XIX of 
the Social Security Act of 1965 as amended. 


MEDICARE. The term Medicare means the program of 
medical care benefits provided under Title XVIII of the 
Social Security Act of 1965 as amended. 


REASONABLE AND CUSTOMARY CHARGE. A charge 
will be considered Reasonable and a Customary if: (1) it is 
the normal charge made by the provider for a similar service 
or supply; and (2) it does not exceed the normal charge 
made by most providers of such service or supply in the 
locality where the service or supply is received. To deter- 
mine if a charge is Reasonable and Customary, the nature 
and severity of the Injury or Sickness being treated will be 
considered. 

EXPENSES INCURRED. An expense is incurred when the 
service or the supply for which it is incurred is provided. 
INJURY. The term Injury means an accidental bodily in- 
jury. 

SICKNESS. The term Sickness means a disease or illness, 
including pregnancy and complications of pregnancy, 
alcoholism, drug abuse, mental or nervous disorders and 
[Section 4 4-6] 


(Continued) 

HOME HEALTH CARE PLAN. The term Home Health 
Care Plan means a plan for care and treatment of a person in 
his home. To qualify, the plan must be established and 
approved in writing by a Physician who certifies that the 
person would require confinement in a Hospital or Skilled 
Nursing Facility if he did not have the care and treatment 
stated in the plan. 


JA-31 


HOME HEALTH CARE AGENCY. The term Home Health 
Care Agency means a Hospital or a non-profit or public 
home health care agency which: 

(1) primarily provides skilled nursing service and other 
therapeutic service under the supervision of a Physi- 
cian or a Registered Graduate Nurse; 

(2) is run according to rules established by a group of 
professional persons; 

(3) maintains clinical records on all patients; 

(4) does not primarily provide custodial care or care and 
treatment of the mentally ill; 


JA-32 


INITIAL EMPLOYEE GROUP. The Initial employee 
Group is made up of Employees: (1) in the employ of an 
Employer on the Effective Date of the policy; and (2) were 
insured under the group medical plan, if any, which was in 
effect on the day immediately preceding the effective day of 
this plan; or (3) in the employ of an Employer on the date 
that Employer becomes an Affiliated Employer. 


NEW EMPLOYEE GROUP. The New Employee Group is 
made up of Employees not in the Initial Employee Group.

[Section 5 (NV-Rev. 8-84) 5-1] 
Each Employee will become eligible for Dependent Insur- 
ance on the latest date below: 
(1) the date he becomes eligible for Employee Insurance: 
(2) the date he acquires his first Dependent; 
(3) the effective date of Dependent Insurance. 
[Section 5 5-2] 
Each Employee may elect to be insured for Employee Insur- 


ance only by signing a payroll deduction form approved by 
the Policyholder and the Insurance Company. The effective 


JA-33 


date of his insurance depends upon the date on which the 
Employee elects the insurance. 


(1) If he elects Employee Insurance on or before the date 
he becomes eligible, his insurance will become effec- 
tive on the first day of the month after he becomes 
eligible. 

(2) If he elects Employee Insurance within 31 days after 
he becomes eligible, his insurance will become effec- 
tive on the first day of the month after he becomes 
eligible or on the date of election, whichever is later. 

(3) If he elects Employee Insurance more than 31 days 
after he becomes eligible, his insurance will become 
effective on the date the Insurance Company agrees in 
writing to insure him. 

(4) If his Employee Insurance ceased because he cancelled 
his payroll deduction, and he again elects to be in- 
sured, his insurance will become effective on the date 
the Insurance Company agrees in writing to insure 
him. 


Under the circumstances described in items 3 and 4, the 
Insurance Company may require the Employee to submit 
evidence of good health acceptable to the Insurance 
Company at his own expense before it agrees to insure 
him. 
If an Employee is not in Active Service on the date his 
insurance would otherwise become effective, it will become 
effective on the date he returns to Active Service. 
[Section 6 6-1] 


Each Employee may elect to be insured for Dependent 
Insurance only by signing a payroll deduction form approved 
by the Policyholder and the Insurance Company. The effec- 


JA-34 


tive date of insurance for each Dependent depends upon the 
date on which the Employee elects Dependent Insurance. 


(1) If the Employee elects Dependent Insurance on or 
before the date he becomes eligible for it, the insur- 
ance for each Dependent will become effective on the 
first day of the month after the Employee becomes 
eligible for it. 


(2) If the Employee elects Dependent Insurance within 31 
days after he becomes eligible for it, the insurance for 
each Dependent will become effective on the first day 
of the month after he becomes eligible or on the date 
of election, whichever is later. 


(3) If the Employee elects Dependent Insurance more than 
31 days after he becomes eligible for it, the insurance 
for each Dependent will become effective on the date 
the Insurance Company agrees in writing to insure the 
Dependent. 


(4) If the Employee’s Dependent Insurance ceased because 
he cancelled his payroll deduction, and he again elects 
to be insured for it, the insurance for each Dependent 
will become effective on the date the Insurance 
Company agrees in writing to insure the Dependent. 

Under the circumstances described in items 3 and 4, the 

Insurance Company may require the Employee, at his 

Own expense, to submit evidence of the Dependent’s good 

health before it agrees to insure the Dependent. 

If a Dependent is a patient in a Hospital on the date his 
insurance would otherwise become effective, the effective 
date of his insurance will be postponed until the day after he 
is discharged from the Hospital. 


An Employee will be insured for Dependent Insurance only 
if he is insured for Employee Insurance. 


JA-35 


Any reference to an insured Dependent means a Dependent 
for whom the Employee is insured. 


EXCEPTIONS FOR NEWBORNS. Any Dependent child 
born while the Employee is insured for Dependent Insurance 
will be insured from the date of birth. A Dependent child 
born while the Employee is insured for himself, but not for 
his Dependents, will become insured on the date of the 
child’s birth, if the Employee elects Dependent Insurance no 
later than 31 days after the child’s birth.

COMPREHENSIVE MEDICAL BENEFITS 


If an Employee or a Dependent incurs Covered Expenses 
while insured for Comprehensive Medical Benefits under 
this policy, the Insurance Company will pay benefits as 
follows, subject to the Benefit Maximums, Co-payment and 
after any Deductible shown in the Schedule: 


(a) [80%] of all Covered Expenses incurred, except that if 
such Covered Expenses are incurred in a Participating 
Hospital, the Insurance Company will pay [90%] of 
those Covered Expenses. If such Covered Expenses 
are incurred in a Non-Participating Hospital, the 
Insurance Company will pay [70%] of those Covered 
Expenses; however, 

(b) the Non-Participating Hospital Deductible shown in 
the Schedule will first be deducted from such Covered 
Expenses for each admission or outpatient surgical 
visit in a Non-Participating Hospital and is exclusive 
of any Personal Expense Limit provisions; except 

(c) when an amount equal to the Calendar Year Deducti- 
ble for each person, if any, has been satisfied in a 
calendar year, the difference between the Calendar 


JA-36 


Year and Non-Participating Hospital Deductibles will 
be deducted for each admission or outpatient surgical 
visit in a Non-Participating Hospital and is exclusive 
of any Personal Expense Limit provisions. 
FULL PAYMENT AREA FOR COVERED EXPENSE 
INCURRED. After a covered person has incurred [$1,000] 
' Of expense for covered charges or a family has incurred 
[$3,000] expense for covered charges during a calendar 
year, excluding expenses used to satisfy the deductible 
provisions of the policy, benefits for that person or family 
will be paid at 100% for the balance of the calendar year. 
However, benefits for expenses incurred for that person for 
or in connection with Mental Iliness, Alcoholism and Drug 
Dependency while not confined in a hospital will continue to 
be payable as provided in the Schedule. 
MAXIMUM BENEFIT PROVISION. The total amount of 
Comprehensive Medical Benefits payable for all expenses 
incurred for an Employee or Dependent in his lifetime will 
not exceed the Maximum Benefit shown in The Schedule. 
However, once a person uses any portion of his Maximum 
Benefit, on each January Ist the Insurance Company will 
reinstate the used amount up to $1,000 to be applied to 
Covered Expenses incurred after the date of reinstatement. 
At any time after the benefits charged to an individual’s 
overall maximum reach a total of at least $1,000, the full 
maximum will be reinstated upon submission to the Insur- 
ance Company of satisfactory proof that the individual is in 
good health.

the Insurance Company will pay for those expenses to the 
same extent as those payable for any other insured Depend- 
ent. Benefits will also be payable for transportation charges 
to and from the nearest available facility staffed and 
equipped to treat the Newborn Dependent’s condition. The 
transportation must be certified by a Physician as medically 
necessary. The maximum amount payable for transportation 
charges for a Newborn Dependent will be no less than 
$1,000. 


In addition to the above, expenses incurred for routine care 
of the Newborn Dependent prior to the date of discharge 
from the Hospital nursery will be deemed to result from a 
Sickness.

congenital defects, birth abnormalities or Premature Birth. 
PREMATURE BIRTH. Premature Birth means a child born 
too early as determined by a Physician.

CO-ORDINATION OF BENEFITS 


If a person covered under this policy (called “this Plan’’) is 
also covered under one or more other Plans, the benefits 
payable for him from this Plan will be co-ordinated with the 
benefits payable for him from all other Plans. 


Co-ordination of Benefits will be used to determine the 
benefits payable for a person for any Claim Determination 
Period if, for the Allowable Expenses incurred in that Peri- 


od, the sum of (a) and (b) below would exceed those Allow- 
able : 


(a) the benefits that would be payable from this Plan 
without co-ordination; and 

(b) the benefits that would be payable from all other Plan 
without Co-ordination of Benefits provisions in those 
Plan. 


The benefits that would be payable from this Plan for 


(b) all the benefits payable for those Allowable Expenses 
pr a 


JA-56 


CO-ORDINATION OF BENEFITS 


(Continued) 


However, the benefits of another Plan will be ignored when 
the benefits of this Plan are determined if: 


(a) the Benefit Determination Rules would require this 
Plan to determine its benefits before that Plan; and 
(b) the other Plan has a provision that co-ordinates its 
benefits with those of this Plan and would, based on 
its rules, determine its benefits after this Plan. 

When Co-ordination of Benefits reduces the total amount 
otherwise payable in a Claim Determination Period for a 
person covered under this Plan, each benefit that would be 
payable in the absence of Co-ordination of Benefits will be 
reduced in proportion. The reduced amount will be charged 

against any applicable benefit limit of this Plan. 

The Insurance Company reserves the right to release to or 
obtain from any other insurance company or other organiza- 
tion or person any information which, in its opinion, it 
needs for the purpose of Co-ordination of Benefits. 

When payments which should have been made under this 
Plan based on the terms of this section have been made 
under any other Plans, the Insurance Company will have the 
right to pay to any organizations making these payments the 
amount it determines to be warranted. Amounts paid in this 
manner will be considered to be benefits paid under this 
Plan. The Insurance Company will be released from all 
liability under this Plan to the extent of these payments. 


When an overpayment has been made by the Insurance 
Company, at any time, it will have the right to recover that 
payment, to the extent of the excess, from the person to 
whom it was made or any other imsurance company or 
organization, as it may determine.

DEFINITIONS 
PLAN. Plan means any: 
(1) group insurance coverage; 
(2) service plan contracts, group of individual practice or 
other prepayment plans; or 
(3) coverage under any labor-management trusteed plans, 
union welfare plans, employer organizations plans, or 
employee benefit organization plans; 
which provides medical or dental benefits or services. It 
does not include coverage under individual or franchise 
policies or contracts. 
Each Plan or part of a Plan which has the right to co- 
ordinate benefits will be considered a separate Plan. 


ALLOWABLE EXPENSE. Allowable Expense means any 
necessary, reasonable and customary item of expense at leas. 
a part of which is covered by any one of the Plans that 
covers the person for whom claim is made. 


When the benefits from a Plan are in the form of services, 
not cash payments, the reasonable cash value of each service 


CLAIM DETERMINATION PERIOD. Claim Determina- 
tion Period means a calendar year or that part of a calendar 
year in which the person has been covered under this Plan.

TO WHOM PAYABLE: All medical benefits are payable to 
the Employee. However, with the consent of the Insurance 
Company and the Policyholder, an insured may direct that 
all or any part of the medical benefits may be paid directly 
to the person or institution on whose charge claim is based. 


If any person to whom benefits are payable is a minor or, in 
the opinion of the Insurance Company, is not able to give 
valid receipt for any payment due him, such payment will be 
made to his legal guardian. However, if no request for 
payment has been made by his legal guardian, the Insurance 
Company may, at its option, make payment to the person or 
Se OD 1 Have EURO Oh cuNIEM and exp 


The Insurance Company will pay benefits for covered serv- 
ices after the insured person has satisfied the deductible and 
other co-insurance amounts he is obligated to pay. Obligated 
to pay means the amount of charges the insured employee or 
his dependents must pay. It does not include any charges 
which are discounted, rebated or waived. The amount of any 
discount, rebate, waived charges or charges the insured 


JA-60 


employee or his insured dependents are not obligated to pay 
shall not be considered covered charges under this contract. 
The Insurance Company shall have the right to recover from 
a provider of services or insured employee any excess bene- 
fit payments made as a result of a discount, rebate, waived 
charges or charges the insured employee or his insured 
dependents are not obligated to pay. 

If an Employee dies while medical benefits remain unpaid, 
the Insurance Company may choose to pay benefits up to 
$3,000 to a person or persons related to the Employee by 
blood or marriage who appears to be entitled to the benefits; 
or the executors of administrators of the Employee's estate. 
The Insurance Company will be discharged to the extent of 
any such payment made in good faith. 


TIME OF PAYMENT OF CLAIMS. Medical benefits will 
be paid by the Insurance Company when it receives proper 
written proof of loss. 


SUBROGATION. In the event of any payment for benefits 
provided to an insured under this policy, the Insurance 
Company, to the extent of such payment, shall be subrogat- 
ed to all rights of recovery such insured has against any 
person or organization. The insured shall execute and deliver 
such instruments and papers as may be required and do 
whatever else is necessary to secure such rights to the Insur- 
ance Company. Where state law eliminates the insureds right 
to recover amounts received from collateral sources, this

TERMINATION OF INSURANCE 


For Employees 


The insurance on an Employee will cease on the earliest date 
below: 


(1) the date the Employee ceases to be in a Class of Eligi- 
ble Employees or ceases to qualify as an Employee; 
(2) the last day for which the Employer has made any re- 

quired contribution for the insurance; 
(3) the date the policy is cancelled; 


(4) the date the Employee's Active Service ends, except as 
set forth below. 


TEMPORARY LAYOFF OR LEAVE OF ABSENCE. If an 
Employee’s Active Service ends due to temporary layoff or 
leave of absence, the insurance will be continued until the 
Policyholder stops paying premium for the Employee or 
otherwise cancels the insurance. However, the insurance will 
not continue for more than 60 days past the date the Em- 
ployee’s Active Service ends. 

INJURY OR SICKNESS. If an Employee’s Active Service 
ends due to an Injury or a Sickness, the insurance will be 
continued for up to 12 months during disability leave or 
absence while the Employee remains totally and continuous- 
ly disabled as a result of the Injury or Sickness. However, 
the insurance will not continue past the date the Policyholder 
stops paying premium for the Employee or otherwise cancels 
the insurance. 
RETIREMENT. If an Employee’s Active Service ends 
because he retires, the insurance will be continued until the 


JA-62 


date on which the Policyholder stops paying premium for the 
Employee or otherwise cancels the insurance. 

Any continuation of insurance must be based on a plan 
which precludes individual selection. 

[Section 12 (NV-Rev. 8/84) 12-1

(Continued) 
For Dependents 
An Employee’s insurance for all of his Dependents will 
cease on the earliest date below: 
(1) the date of the Employee’s insurance for himself 
ceases, except in the case of death; 
(2) the date the Employee ceases to be in a class of 
Employees eligible for Dependent Insurance, 
(3) the last day for which the Employee has made any re- 
quired contribution for Dependent Insurance, 
(4) the date Dependent Insurance is cancelled. 
An Employee’s insurance for any one of his Dependents will 
cease on the date the Dependent no longer qualifies as a 
Dependent.

(1) the last day of the twelfth month after the Employee's

MEDICAL CONVERSION PRIVILEGE 
For Employees and Dependents 


When an Employee’s or a Dependent’s Medical Expense 
Insurance ceases, he may be eligible to be insured under an 
individual policy or certificate of medical care benefits 
(called the Converted Policy) issued by the Insurance 
Company. A Converted Policy will be issued by the Insur- 
ance Company only to a person who is Entitled to Convert, 
and only if he applies in writing and pays the first premium 
for the Converted Policy to the Insurance Company within 
31 days after the date his insurance ceases. Evidence of good 
health is not needed. The Insurance Company will provide 
all necessary information to a person who is Entitled to 
Convert, so that application may be made. 

EMPLOYEES ENTITLED TO CONVERT. An Employee 
is entitled to Convert Medical Expense Insurance for himself 
and all of his Dependents who were insured when his insur- 
ance ceased, provided such persons have been continuously 
insured for at least 3 months under this policy and any group 
policy providing similar benefits which it replaced, except a 
Dependent who is eligible for Medicare or would be Overin- 
sured, but only if: 

(1) The Employee’s insurance ceased because he was no 
longer in Active Service or no longer eligible for 
Medical Expense Insurance. 

(2) The Employee does not replace this group plan within 
31 days by another group plan. 


JA-65 


(3) The Employee is not eligible for Medicare. 

(4) The Employee would not be Overinsured. 
A retired Employee may apply for a Converted Polic 
within 31 days after his retirement date in place of any 
continuation of his insurance that may be available under 
this plan when he retires, if he is otherwise Entitled to 

vert.

OVERINSURED. A person will be considered Overinsured

other plan or program; or (b) those for which the 
person is eligible, whether or not covered, under any

basis; or (c) those available for the person by or 
through any state, provincial or federal law.

basis. The choice of policies offered will include a policy

Company may provide a Medicare Supplement policy for a 
person eligible for Medicare.

Converted Policy will be issued to each Dependent who is 
Entitled to Convert. The Converted Policy will take effect 
on the day after the person’s insurance under this plan 
ceases. The premium on its effective date will be based on: 
(a) class of risk and age; and (b) benefits.

If the Policyholder and the Insurance Company agree to a 
change in the method of premium payment or to a change in 
the Anniversary Date, a pro rata adjustment will be made in 
the premium due. 


CHANGES IN PREMIUM RATES. Any premium rate may 
be changed by the Insurance Company from time to time 
with at least 31 days advance written notice. No change in 
rates will be made until 12 months after the Effective Date. 
An increase in rates will not be made more often than once 
in a 12 month period. 


However, the Insurance Company may change rates imme- 
diately if, in its opinion, its liability is altered by any change 
in state or federal law or by a revision in the insurance under 
the policy. Any such change in rates will take effect on the 
effective date of the change in law or change in the insur- 
ance. 


If an increase in rates takes place on a date that is not a 
Premium Due Date, a pro rata premium will be due on the 
date of the increase. The pro rata premium will apply for the 
increase from the date of the increase to the next Premium 
Due Date. If a decrease in rates takes place on a data that is 
not a Premium Due Date, a pro rata credit will be granted. 
The pro rata credit will apply for the decrease from the date 
of the decrease to the next Premium Due Date. 


JA-70 


Insurance Company grants a credit, the excess, if any, of the 
policyholder fund paid for the coverage including the cost of 
Administration, shall be applied for the benefit of insured 
employees or members.

CANCELLATION OF POLICY 


The Policyholder may cancel the policy as of any Premium 
Due Date by giving written notice to the Insurance Company 
before that date. 


The Insurance Company may cancel the policy as of any 
Premium Due Date if the number of insured Employees is 
less than 25 or less than 75% of those eligible. Dependent 
Insurance may be cancelled as of any Premium Due Date if 
the number of Employees insured for their Dependents is 
less than 75% of those eligible. 

If a premium is not paid when due, the policy will automati- 
cally be cancelled as of the Premium Due Date, except as set 
forth below. 


GRACE PERIOD. If, before a Premium Due Date, the 
Policyholder has not given written notice to the Insurance 
Company that the policy is to be cancelled, a Grace Period 
of 31 days will be granted for the payment of each premium 
after the initial premium. The policy will stay in effect 
during that time. If any premium is not paid by the end of 
the Grace Period; except that, if the Policyholder has given 
written notice in advance of an earlier date of cancellation, 
the policy will be cancelled as of the earlier date. The Poli- 
cyholder will be liable to the Insurance Company for any 
unpaid premium for the time the policy was in force. 

[Section 16 (NV-Rev. 8/84) 16-1]

NOTICE OF CLAIM. Written notice of claim must be 
given to the Insurance Company within 30 days after the 
occurrence or start of the loss on which claim is based. 

If notice is not given in that time, the claim will not be 
invalidated or reduced if it is shown that written notice was 
given as soon as was reasonable possible. 

CLAIM FORMS. When the Insurance Company receives 
the notice of claim, it will give to the claimant, or to the 
Policyholder for claimant, the claim forms it used for filing 
proof of loss. If the claimant does not get these claim forms 
within 15 days after the Insurance Company receives notice 
of claim, he will be considered to have met the proof or loss 
requirements if he submits written proof of loss within 90 
days after the date of loss. This proof must describe the 
occurrence, character and extent of the loss for which claim 
is made.

MEDICAL QUALITY COST MANAGEMENT RIDER 
PRE-ADMISSION REVIEW 


A physician must obtain prior verification from us that any 
non-emergency admission of an insured person to a hospital 
for surgical or medical care is medically necessary as de- 
fined by the Group Policy. If we determine that the admis- 
sion is not medically necessary, but the insured person elects 
to receive the prescribed care, it will not be covered under 
the Group Policy. 


If the physician does not contact us before the insured 
person is admitted, we will review the admission on the first 
working day following the admission. If we determine that 
the admission is not medically necessary, we will notify the 
insured person in writing that coverage will end in 24 hours. 
If the insured person chooses to remain in the hospital 
beyond that time, any expenses the insured person incurs 
will be his or her responsibility. 


CONTINUED STAY REVIEW 

We will review a stay after an insured person is admitted to 
the hospital. If continued stay in the hospital is determined 
not to be medically necessary, the insured person will be 
notified in writing that coverage will end in 24 hours. If the 
insured person chooses to remain in the hospital beyond that 
time, the insured person will have to pay any remaining 
expenses that he or she incurs. 


JA-75 


SURGICAL NECESSITY 


If a physician recommends that an insured person have 
elective, non-emergency surgery, the physician must obtain 


responsible for any expenses incurred for or in connection 
with the surgery.

18. The Co-Payor Class consists of employees obtaining 
health ineurance benefits under the employee benefit plan as 
defined under 29 U.S.C. 1001 ef seq. 


19. The members of the Co-Payor Class incurred charges 
at Humana Hospital Sunrise for hospitalization and were 
obligated to or did pay a portion of the hospital charges. 
Defendant Humana-Insurance is the insurer of the members 
of the Co-Payor Class through its insurance plan, Humana 
Care Plus. | 

20. The Humana Care Plus policy required that each 
member of the Co-Payor Class pay 20% of hospital costs. 
Humana-Insurance agreed to pay the remaining 80% of the 
hospital charges. The Co-Payor Class believed, and their 
agreement with Humana-Insurance provided, that the 
members of the Co-Payor Class and Humana-Insurance 
would be paying their respective contractual percentage of 
the charges. 

. Humana and Humana-Insurance negotiated a secret 
agreement ‘which greatly reduced or discounted hospital 
charges for Humana-Insurance patients incurring charges at 
Humana Hospital Sunrise which constitutes discretionary 
of the plan, discretionary authority and discretionary control 
respecting management or disposition of its assets, or discre-

ary duties to all members of the Co-Payor Class in that it 
has failed to pass along the reduced costs it was able to 
negotiate with Humana in the form of reduced co-payments. 
29 U.S.C. § 1004.

SECOND CLAIM FOR RELIEF 
(Anti-Trust) 


38. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every allega- 
tion contained in paragraphs 1 through 37 of the Complaint 
as though fully set forth at this point. . 


39. This Premium-Payor Class consists of individuals or 
entities paying all or a portion of the Humana-Insurance 
premiums during the period 1985 to 1988, including but not 
limited to employers who made insurance premium pay- 
ments on behalf of their employees, as well as individual 
employees who either directly paid insurance premiums on 
behalf of their dependents, or who did so indirectly by such 
means as payroll deductions by their employers. 


JA-88 


40. Defendant Humana is in the business of owning and 
operating for-profit acute care health facilities. Humana 
owns and operates a for-profit acute care health facility 
named Humana-Sunrise Hospital which provides a full range 
of hospital services within Clark County, Nevada. Approxi- 
mately sixty percent (60%) of the gross charges for all 
patients hospitalized in major for-profit acute care facilities 
in Clark County, Nevada were billed by Humana-Sunrise 
Hospital in the relevant time period. This percentage is, 
and at all times relevant hereto has been, the largest market 
share of any for profit acute care facility in Clark County, 
Nevada. 


41. At all times relevant hereto, the hospital charges 
billed by Humana for hospitalization at Humana-Sunrise 
were anti-competitively high compared to comparable geo- 
graphic markets. In 1986, the State of Nevada had the 
highest per patient day billed charges in the United States 
and Humana-Sunrise had the highest per patient day billed 
charges in the State of Nevada; i.e., $1,617 per patient day. 
At all times relevant hereto, the percentage return on in- 
vestment at Humana-Sunrise was anti-competitively high 
compared to comparable geographic markets. In 1986, 
Humana-Sunrise possessed a 37.44% return on investment. 
At all times relevant hereto, the profit margin of Humana- 
Sunrise was anti-competitively high compared to comparable 
geographic markets. In 1986, Humana-Sunrise possessed a 
profit margin of 36% compared to a national average of 
14.4%. 


42. In 1984, Humana, through its wholly-owned subsidi- 
ary, GROUP HEALTH, began to market and sell group 
health insurance policies in Clark County, Nevada. Afore- 
said policies were sold through organized advertising activi- 
ties in Clark County, Nevada, by means of newspaper adver- 
tisements, radio advertisements and television advertise- 
ments. GROUP HEALTH marketed and sold such policies 


JA-89 


using public advertising and private lici . In ilizi 

the above-described marketing scheme, Humana did so for 
the primary purpose of maintaining Humana’s dominant 
market share of the gross charges from all patients hospital- 
ized in major for-profit acute care facilities in Clark County, 
Nevada by maintaining or increasing the number of patients 
utilizing Humana-Sunrise. 

44. Humana and Humana-Insurance controlled the pricing 
mechanisms for the per diem charges for inpatient hospitali- 
zation and hospital services billed by Humana and for 
Humana group health policies. Humana and Humana- 
Insurance engaged in predatory pricing to give Humana an 
unlawful competitive advantage by allowing it to maintain, 
through revenues transmitted to its subsidiary by secret 
discounts, an anti-competitively high profit by maintaining 
Or imcreasing the number of patients and in addition, by 
providing Humana-Insurance with a significantly lower per 
diem charge. As a result of aforesaid unlawful efforts to 
maintain Or increase its market share and also as a result of 
mere ge) te Naan dimrseenagy yr gponnd 

maintained overwhe percentage of the 
charges for all patients hospitalized in major for-profit acute 
care facilities in Clark County, Nevada. 


45. The imsurance agreement sold and marketed by 
Humana-Insurance through public advertising and private 
solicitation required its insureds to pay all expenses up to the 
designated deductible amount, and twenty percent (20%) of 
all expenses beyond the deductible; Humana-Insurance was 
required to pay the remaining ei 80% 
aa Aa eighty percent (80%) beyond 

46. Humana (Humana-Sunrise) and Humana-Insurance 
entered into a secret agreement in 1984 pursuant to which 
Humana-Insurance would pay Humana a discounted amount 


JA-90 


for the portion of the per diem hospital charges for which it 
was responsible. No comparable discount was provided to 
other group health insurers. No comparable discount was 
provided on that portion of the charges paid by the insureds. 
Humana and Humana-Insurance agreed to keep this agree- 
ment secret from the Premium-Payor Class, the general 
public and state agencies. 

47. Since at least 1984, and to and including the date of 
the filing of this Complaint, Humana and Humana-Insurance 
have violated Section 2 of the Sherman Act (15 U.S.C. § 2) 
by monopolizing or attempting to monopolize the provision 
of major for-profit acute care facilities in Clark County, 
Nevada. 


48. In furtherance of the aforesaid monopolization and 
attempt to monopolize, Defendant Humana and Humana- 
Insurance have developed a mutually supportive system to 
engage in specific unlawful acts and practices for the express 
purpose of maintaining anti-competitively high hospital

THIRD CLAIM FOR RELIEF 
(18 U.S.C. § 1961-1968) 


54. Plaintiffs reassert and reallege each and every obliga- 
tion contained in paragraphs: 1 through 52 of the Amended 
Complaint as though fully set fourth at this point. 


55. As part of a scheme to defraud Plaintiffs by charging 
Plaintiffs a different per diem per patient day on their co- 
payments, Humana and Humana-Insurance entered into a 
secret agreement (the ‘‘Agreement’’). Pursuant to the 
Agreement, Humana-Insurance agreed to pay Sunrise Hospi- 
tal a discounted amount for that portion of the hospital 
charges for which it was responsible. The portion of the 
charges paid by others, however, was not discounted but 
was still based on the usual and customary rate. 


56. Premiums were not reduced by Humana-Insurance in 
the amount discounted. It was part of the scheme to defraud 


mation important to the insureds (including but not limited 
to the existence of the secret agreement and the fact that 
Humana was billing Humana-Insurance at a different per 
diem) all so as to unlawfully, intentionally and wilfully, and 
with the intent to defraud, that is, knowingly and with spe- 
cific intent to deceive in order to cause financial gain to 
themselves, procure secret profits to the use and benefit of 
themselves and others and to the detriment of the insureds 
consisting of the named Plaintiffs, as representatives of a 
class pursuant to Fed.R.Civ.Pro. Rule 23. 


57. The scheme to defraud evolved over time a pattern of 
illegal activity that inflicted discreet harms on the insureds 
and others. The insureds, as victims of the unlawful patterns 


Humana-Insurance engaged, inter alia, in conduct in viola- 
tion of federal laws, to wit: 18 U.S.C. Section 1341: 18 
U.S.C. Section 1343; and 18 U.S.C. § 664, as stated below: 


(A) 18 U.S.C. Section 1341. As part of the false and 
fraudulent scheme to defraud, Defendant Humana and/or 
Defendant Humana-Insurance, caused to be sent through the 
United States mail explanations of benefits, patient account 
Statements, itemized statements, demand letters and other 


of facts and information irom the insureds was intentionally 
and knowingly conceived in order to deceive the insured and 
obtain financial gain. This use of the United States mail in 
furtherance of the false and fraudulent scheme violates 18 
U.S.C. Section 1341. 


(B) 18 U.S.C. Sections 1343. As part of the false and 
fraudulent scheme to defraud, Defendant Humana and/or 
Defendant Humana-Insurance, caused to be sent through 
Humavia group health policy as part of the false and fraudu- 
lent scheme to defraud, Defendants further used the tele- 
phone lines to explain or discuss billing in order to collect 
co-payments from insureds for hospital charges at Sunrise 


JA-%6 


Hospital. Defendant Humana and/or Defendant Humana- 
Humana-Insurance entered into an agreement specifically 
providing for the insurance company to pay the hospital 
based on a discounted rate while the insureds were to pay 
based on the usual and customary rate. This material nondis- 
closure and concealment of fact and information to the 
insureds was intentionally and knowingly conceived in order 
to deceive the insured and obtain financial gain. This vio- 
lates 18 U.S.C. Section 1343. 

(C) 18 U.S.C. § 664. As part of the false and fraudulent 
scheme the defraud, Defedant Humana and/or Defendant 
Humana-Insurance unlawfully and willfully converted co- 
payments and premiums to their own use. By failing to bill 
the co-payor class at a discounted rate, Humana converted 
these funds to its own use. By failing to pass along premium 
reductions, Humana-Insurance converted these funds to its 
own use. 18 U.S.C. § 664 explicitly provides that any 
person who ‘‘unlawfully and willfully abstracts or converts 
to his own use or to the use of another, any of the... 
premiums . . . of any employee welfare plan . . . .”” shall be 
fined not more than $10,000, or imprisoned not more than 
five years, or both.

premium of approximately $355.00 per month. Her hus-

Insurance policy. Robert Cade was hospitalized at Humana 
Hospital Sunrise between October 7, 1987 and October 17, 
1987. The Cades incurred a hospital bill of $16,437.66. 
Mrs. Cade paid co-payments for her husband’s hospitaliza- 
tion.

11. Plaintiff Searle Auto Glass, Inc., doing business as 
Best Glass Co. (‘‘Best Glass’’), 1225 S. Commerce Street, 
Las Vegas, Nevada 89102, is a Nevada corporation in good 
standing. Best Glass has utilized Humana-Insurance to 
provide health insurance to its employees between the years 
1985 and 1988, inclusive. Best Glass’ group number with 
Humana-Insurance is 82801-18351-001. When Best Glass 
first became an insured with Humana-Insurance, the rates 
were very reasonable, $64.23 per employee per billing 
period. In 1986 this rate increased by 3% and in 1987 the 
rate jumped 13%. Finally, in 1988, when Best Glass decided 
not to renew with Humana-Insurance, Humana-Insurance 
sought a premium increase of 41% to continue coverage of 
employees of Best Glass. At no time did Humana-Insurance

26. Defendant Humana-Insurance has breached its fiduci- 
ary duties to all members of the Co-Payor Class in that it 
has failed to pass along the reduced costs it was able to 
negotiate with Humana in the form of reduced co-payments. 
29 U.S.C. § 1004. 


27. Defendant Humana-Insurance has breached its fiduci- 
ary duties to all members of the Co-Payor Class in that it 
has failed to refund the discount of the reduced costs it was 
able to negotiate with Humana. 29 U.S.C. § 1104. 


28. Defendant Humana-Insurance has breached its fiduci- 
ary duties to all members of the Co-Payor Class by breach- 
ing its duty to accomplish the exclusive purpose of providing 
benefits to participants and by failing to defray the reason- 
able expenses necessary for the administration of the plan. 
Humana-Insurance imprudently decided to retain the co- 
payment refunds it negotiated with Humana. Humana- 
Insurance received excessive compensation in violation of its 
fiduciary duties. 29 U.S.C. § 1104. 

29. Defendant Humana-Insurance has breached its fiduci- 
ary duties to all members of the Co-Payor Class by failing to 
adhere to the documents and instruments governing the plan, 
including but not limited to, AB 289. 29 U.S.C. § 1104. 


30. Defendant Humana-Insurance engaged in prohibited 
transactions in violation of its fiduciary duties by dealing 
with the assets of the plan in its own interest or for its own 
account. 29 U.S.C. § 1106. 


31. Defendant Humana-Insurance engaged in prohibited 
transactions in violation of its fiduciary duties by acting on 
behalf of Humana whose interests are adverse to the interests 
of the plan or the Co-Payor Class. Humana-Insurance was 
prohibited from even negotiating the co-payment amount 
with its related entity Humana. 29 U.S.C. § 1106. 


JA-119 


32. Defendant Humana-Insurance engaged in prohibited 
transactions in violation of its fiduciary duties by unlawfully 
receiving consideration for its own personal account in 
connection with a transaction involving the assets of the 
plan. 29 U.S.C. § 1106. 

33. It would be wrongful for Humana-Insurance to retain 
monies, in excess of those it might otherwise have been 
entitled to, when such monies have been obtained wrongful- 
ly and in breach of Humana-Insurance’s duties to the Co- 
Payor Class and agreement with the Co-Payor Class. 


34. A constructive trust should be imposed on the De- 
fendants which requires turnover of all monies obtained 
through the scheme described herein. 


35. Plaintiffs should be named the beneficiaries of the 
constructive trust awarded by this Court. 


36. As a result of the Defendants’ unlawful and/or inequi- 
table scheme heretofore alleged, Plaintiffs have conferred a 
monetary benefit upon Defendant Humana-Insurance. De- 
fendant Humana-Insurance appreciated and realized the 
monetary benefit and accepted and retained the monetary 
benefit under circumstances such that it would be inequitable 
for Humana-Insurance to retain the monetary benefit without 
returning the benefit to the Co-Payor Class. As a result of 
the scheme, the Defendant Humana-Insurance was unjustly 
enriched, all to the detriment of the Co-Payor Class.

forth and described herein are reasonably likely to continue 
in the future and cause further injury and damage to Plain-

41. This Premium-Payor class consists of individuals or

means as payroll deductions by their employers.

owns and operates a for-profit acute care health facility 
named Humana-Sunrise Hospital which provides a full range 
of hospital services within Clark County, Nevada. Approxi- 
mately sixty percent (60%) of the gross charges for all 
patients hospitalized in major for-profit acute care facilities 
in Clark County, Nevada were billed by Humana-Sunrise 
Hospital in the relevant time period. This percentage is, and 
at all times relevant hereto has been, the largest market share 
of any for profit acute care facility in Clark County, Neva-

charges for which it was responsible. The portion of the 
charges paid by others, however, was not discounted but 
was still based on the usual and customary rate.

AFFIDAVIT OF BARON T. YOUN 


I, Baron T. Youn, bei 
, : oun, being duly sworn, depose and state as 


calculate their financial liability in this case. I 
have also reviewed the deposition and affidavit testimony of 
Bruce McClure and Joseph Shutts. 


2. The data I was instructed to review was supplied on 
magnetic tape and supplied in the EBCIDIC format. This 
data was actually a series of reports that were run and the 
output data was directed to a data file instead of being di- 
rected to a printer. This re-direction to a data file was neces- 
sary because the reports contain over 220,000 transactions. 
The EBCIDIC encoded data on the magnetic tape was 
converted to ASCII format and transferred to media that is 
capable of being read on an IBM PC compatible personal 


JA-142 


computer. This conversion process uses was performed by 
the Shafstall Corp. located in Indianapolis, Indiana and 
specialists in data format conversion. The conversion does 
not alter any values of the data, only the format. 

3. Upon careful study and review of the data submitted by 
Humana, I have found several errors in their calculations. 
For example, on page one of the report labeled ‘HUMANA 
DIFFERENCE FOR NEVADA MEMBERS, DETAIL 
REPORT FOR THE CALENDAR YEAR ENDING ON 
12/31/85’, I have found two separate transactions that are 
in error out of the twelve transactions listed on that page. If 
this error rate is multiplied across the 220,000 plus transac- 
tions, any calculations using this data set will be flawed. 
Since this data set is alleged to be the same data set Humana 
used to figure their liability, their resultant sum they arrived 
at using this data set must be disregarded because of the 
errors contained in the original data set.

and correct to the best of my knowledge, information and 
belief.

JA-160 


0. Summary of Opinion 
A. ERISA Analysis 


I have reviewed 29 U.S.C. § 1002(21), and understand 
that a person is a fiduciary with respect to an employee 
benefits insurance plan if that person exercises any discre- 
tionary authority or discretionary control respecting man- 
agement of such plan, or exercises any authority or control 
respecting management or disposition of the plan’s assets, or 
if the person has any discretionary authority or responsibility 
in the administration of the insurance plan. 

I further understand that Judge Pro has ruled that the 
insurance contracts themselves are assets of the insurance 
plan. With that understanding in mind, and after reviewing 
the master contract, HCP-NV-84-1 and the Preferred Pro- 
vider Agreement and its amendments (the Secret 
Agreement), I am of the opinion that, from an economic 
point of view, the Secret Agreement payment terms, specifi- 
cally ‘‘Attachment B’’ to the Agreement, did have an 
economic affect on the payment provisions of the insurance 
contracts. 


I am further of the opinion that the payment terms of 
Attachment B materially changed the financial obligation of 
Humana Insurance from what it was otherwise obligated to 
pay pursuant to the payment terms of the insurance contract. 


I have also reviewed 29 U.S.C. § 1104(a)(1), and under- 
stand that an ERISA fiduciary must discharge its duties with 
respect to the insurance plan solely in the interests of the 
participants and beneficiaries, in accordance with the plan 
contracts. 

I am of the opinion that a payment of hospital charges 
based on a percentage or a per diem to Sunrise Hospital by 
Humana Insurance according to ‘‘Attachment B’’ of the 
Secret Agreement was not in accordance with the payment 


JA-161 


terms of the insurance contracts. Furthermore, I am of the 
opinion that payment of hospital charges based on a percent- 
age or a per diem to Sunrise Hospital according to ‘‘At- 
tachment B’’ of the Secret Agreement while calculating the 
working person's payment on full billed charges was not in 
the best financial interest of the working person. 


Based on the ERISA statutes and the holding of Jud 

in his July 8, aNd: Gelur tae Gis eoauete ones tu eaaane 
to the benefit of the discount arrangement, the above refer- 
enced financial information of the Defendants was reviewed 
to determine the full amount of the discount during the class 
years 1985, 1986, 1987 and 1988.

1. Since the Second Amended Complaint was filed I have 
calculated the discount received by Humana Insurance from 


Sunrise Hospital as set forth below. 
1985: $ 5,058,000.00 
1986: $15,621,000.00 
1987: $15,743,000.00 
1988: $13,018,000.00 


The total of all such discounts for the class period f 
1985 - 1988 equals $49,440,000.00. .

that period, Defendants’ sworn statement would have gone 
unchallenged.
